DEPARTMENT OF TOWN BOARD
 
  
Town government is run by the town board as the executive, admi
nistrative and legislative body of 
the town.  The board acts as 
a unit. 
Each town board member has no more or no less authority than
 any other board member.  The town board may, by resolution, 
delegate to the supervisor the power a
nd duties of administration of town to be
 performed on behalf of the town board. 
 Meetings of the town board are scheduled 
every two weeks during the year.  Executive 
Sessions and Work Sessions are scheduled a
s 
required.  Town board members are elected for a term of office of four year
s.  Election of members is norm
ally staggered, so that the whol
e 
town board will not come up for election in the same year.

•
 
Expedite the applications and review process for 
maintenance and improvement construction projects

DEPARTMENT OF FINANCE
 
  The Director of Finance and her staff are 
responsible for processing payments for al
l purchases and for c
ontractual obligations
 of the 
Town, preparing and maintaining payroll reco
rds and administering Town trust funds a
nd debt service. The Finance Department 
invests all available funds to meet the cash flow needs of the To
wn. As the interest earned on these investments is a major sou
rce of 
revenue for the Town, timeliness in investi
ng available funds is imperative. Operations
 in the office include receipts, disburs
ements, 
payroll administration, and budget management. 
 The Director of Finance provides genera
l oversight and advice to the Supervisor
 and 
Town Board members.  The Department of
 Finance provides budget reminders and year-t
o-date expenditure figures monthly to 
department heads.

DEPARTMENT OF THE RECEIVER OF TAXES
 
  The  office  of  the  North  Castle  Receiver  of  
Taxes  is  responsible  for  the  tax  collection  
of  five  (5)  School  
Districts  which  inclu
de: 
Bedford, Byram Hills, Valhalla, Harrison and 
Mount Pleasant, along with the collection of
 Town, County and Special Districts.  
The 
Receiver of Taxes Department also collects for five (5) Water Di
stricts.  Records regarding new owners, escrow accounts, banks,
 and 
anything pertaining to the accuracy of a tax bill are continually
 updated.  We handle an abundanc
e of telephone calls from tax 
payers, 
real estate agents, title companies and a
ttorneys at closings.  We send a welcoming 
“Newcomer Packet” to each new homeowner upo
n 
arrival.    A  file  card  for  every  parcel  located  in  North  Castle  
with  parcel  identification,  name,  
address,  water  district/number
  (if  
applicable) and anything pertaining to the 
parcel is maintained by the Receiver of Ta
xes. Our primary goal is to welcome and pr
ovide 
each taxpayer with courteous, professional and prompt service.

DEPARTMENT OF POLICE
 
 In addition to the following financial goals, 
the department supervisors and the Chief 
meet and establish yearly progress goals
 for the 
use within the department.  A set of goals has 
been established every year since the year 2000. 
  
Mission Statement
 
 
The Mission of the North Castle Police Depart
ment is to enhance the quali
ty of life in our town by working in partnership with 
the 
community and in accordance with constitutional rights, to enfor
ce laws, preserve the peace, reduce fear, and provide a safe 
environment.  
2006 Accomplishments of the Department of Police
  
  Police Department:
 
 
•
 
Increased staffing within the Patrol Divi
sion in order to provide more effective 
service to the citizens of North Castle. 
 
•
 
Responded to 11,954 calls for service from th
e community resulting in 205 arrests 
 
•
 
Provided traffic enforcement resulting I 
the issuance of 2,025 total summons (954 St
ate Uniform Traffic Tickets and 1,071 
Town Summons) 
 
•
 
Supported the operations and endeavors of all Town 
Departments and Fire Dist
ricts whenever possible 
 
•
 
Continued to maintain the Police Departme
nt’s New York State Accred
itation program in an effort to decrease town and 
department liability, which should have the same positive 
effect on town insurance rates over the course of 2007. 
  
•
 
Continued to maintain, train, and deploy spec
ialized units further enhancing the safety
 of our police officers and the members 

of the community served 
 
•
 
Worked with numerous community groups to study traffic conditions
 in an attempt to improve the safety of town highways, 
roadways and within 
residential neighborhoods 
 
•
 
Worked closely with the Byram Hills Sc
hool District to improve the required S
AVE legislation drills and activities

Maintained the highest standards of law enforcement professi
onalism in accordance with the 
Department’s Mission Statement 
  Community Relations:
 
 
•
 
Completed the second Youth Police Academy 
Program in conjunction with the Byram H
ills High School Apprentice Program.  
The program is a four-week intensive training program offered to
 senior year students at Byram Hills High School to expose 
them to a career in law enforcement. 
 •
 
Continued to actively support numerous community relations progr
ams, most notably the Child Safety Seat Program.  This 
program operates to ensure that child safety
 seats are correctly installed in vehicles
.  These professional installs ensure a b
etter 
survival rate in vehicle collisions. 
  Emergency Operations:
 
 
•
 
Completed all phases of implementation as set forth in the Un
ited States Department of Ju
stice Technical Grant Program. 
These funds were applied for and used to co
mplete the Police Departments Special Operati
ons Trailer. This tr
ailer is in service
 
and can be used to support numerous types of

ng relationship with the Town
's Emergency Manager. Th
is relationship has better 
prepared the Department to respond and ope
rate at the scene of an emergency in

06.  This  support  included  the

DEPARTMENT OF RECREATION AND PARKS
 
  The North Castle Recreation and Parks Depart
ment is dedicated to providing superior 
quality recreation programs and services to
 all 
residents of North Castle regardless of age, economic or social 
status.  Through our programs we seek to strengthen families, p
romote 
wellness, build self esteem, and enhance the 
quality of life for all reside
nts in our community.  As stewards of our park syste
m we are 
committed to preserving the nearly 350 acres of parkland for future
 generations to behold and enjoy.  We will strive to maintai
n and 
improve all of our facilities to keep them safe and enjoyable fo
r all, as well as preserve and promote the appreciation of our 
historic 
and natural resources.  The people who provi
de our services are our greatest resource
.  The North Castle Recreation and Parks 
Department strives to employ the best qualif
ied staff members and to encourage person
al development through in-service and othe
r 
professional training opportunities.

DEPARTMENT OF PLANNING
 
 The Planning Department provide
s professional and administrative 
services related to the develo
pment process for the Planning B
oard 
and  Town  Board.    These  services  include  th
e  review  and  administration  
of  subdivisions,  site  plans  
and  state  environmental  quali
ty 
review.    In  addition,  when  needed,  the  Plan
ning  Department  provides  prof
essional  services  to  the  Zoni
ng  Board  of  Appeals,  the  
Landmarks  Preservation  Committee  and  the  Arch
itectural  Board  of  Review.    The  Planning  
Department  also  plays  a  large  role  in  
disseminating  information  to  the  public  by  responding  to  e-mail  a
nd  telephone  inquires  regarding  applications  before  the  variou
s 
Town approving agencies.  
2006 Accomplishments of the Department of Planning
 
 •
 
Assisted in preparation of Moratori
um Planning and Zoning Study Draft Gene
ric Environmental Impact Statement 
•
 
Assisted Town in becoming a Hudson Valley Greenway Compact Community 
•
 
Created a local law to amend Section 213-15.C(7) of the Town of 
North Castle Town Code to el
iminate the special use permit 
and public hearing requirements for th
e installation of a temporary sign 
•
 
Created a local law to
 amend Section 213-21 of the Town of North Castle 
Town Code to permit Auto Impound (Tow Storage) 
Lots  as  a  special  use  within  the  IND-A  Zoning  District  and  to
  amend  Section  213-33R  of  the  
Town  of  North  Castle  Town  
Code to create appropriate special us
e permit standards for Auto Impound Lots 
•
 
Created a local law to amend Chapter 107 (Fir
e Prevention) of the Town of
 North Castle Town Code to require installation of 
Key boxes on new commercial buildings 
and new commercial gated driveways 
•
 
Created a local law to
 amend Section 213-21 of the Town of North Castle 
Town Code to permit taxi and limousine dispatch 
facilities as a permitted principal use within th
e PLI, RELIP, IND-AA an
d IND-A Zoning Districts 
•
 
Created a local law to amend Section A216 
of North Castle Town Code to include
 specific standards for private roads 
•
 
Served on various Town Board special committees including Ar
monk Hamlet Task Force and the Capital Improvement Task 
Force  
•
 
Converted all application fo
rms into digital format capable of be
ing downloaded from the Town’s website 
•
 
Posted future Planning Board m
eeting agendas on Town website 
•
 
Posted recently adopted Planni
ng Board minutes on Town website

 memoranda page for most recent Planni
ng Board meeting linked to Town website

dent Management System classes 
(emergency management training)

BOARD OF CONSERVATION
 
  
The Conservation Board consists of nine unpaid volunteer member
s who serve two year terms.  
Originally established as a 
Conservation Advisory Council in 1970, the 
Town Board created the Conservation Board 
in 1975 under the leader
ship of Betsy Slud
er 
who served as its Chair until 2001.  The Board 
functions in an advisory capacity to th
e Town Board and other boards and assists
 in the 
development of sound planning policies to help 
assure protection of 
our natural resources. 
 It reviews all development proposals of 10 or
 more acres and all wetland permit appli
cations in coordination with the Planning 
Board and the Town Engineer.  The focus of
 present efforts is preparation of educat
ional information and 
study relating to prot
ection 
of surface and groundwater resources 
and the preservation of open space. 
  
2006 Accomplishments of the Board of Conservation
 
 
•
 
Review referrals from the Planning Bo
ard and Town Engineer; since year 2000, 
212 Wetland Permits have been issued -- 
approximately 30 per year, 19 to date in 2006 
•
 
Continued water testing program for four 
locations along the East Branch of the 
Mianus River for a final report to be 
completed this fall 
•
 
Prepared Guidelines for Wetland Permit App
lications for applicant and consultant 
information.  It identifies procedures, 
process, and schedules.  Permits are require
d for disturbance of land in or within 
100 feet of a wetland, stream or water body 
•
 
Preparing informational flyers for septic
 system management, lawn care, pestic
ides and household chemicals, and energy 
conservation.  This information to town residents is also pa
rt of the stormwater management
 requirements of the New York 
State Department of Environmental 
Conservation Stormwater II Program 
•
 
Continued cooperation with town 
departments and organizations 
•
 
Served as member, Kensico Environmental Educational Program 
•
 
Served as member, New York State Asso
ciation of Conservation Commissions  
•
 
Served as member, Westchester Count
y Environmental Management Council  
•
 
Served as member, Federated Conserva
tionists of Westchester County, Inc.  
•
 
Served as Town representative to the West
chester County Bronx River Watershed Coalition 
 

2007 Goals of the Board of Conservation
 
 
•
 
Complete the preparation and pr
inting of hand-out brochures on e
nvironmental subjects, includi
ng lawn care, pesticides and 
household chemicals, energy conservation, a
nd protections for wells and aquifers 
•
 
Update the Town of North Castle
 Environmental Features Map  
•
 
Identify environmentally sensitive pa
rcels of land related to
 aquifer and water supply
 protection following the 
recommendations of the North Castle Open Space Study 
•
 
Continue water testing of the Mianus, By
ram and Wampus Rivers, compare with past
 testing, and prepare 
a report of findings 
•
 
Review of Wetland Permit Applications with
 continued effort to coor
dinate and simplify procedures for small projects and 
provide sufficient expert
ise for large projects 
•
 
Provide educational opportunities for Cons
ervation Board members and promote inte
rest in future Conservation Board 
membership

DEPARTMENT OF HIGHWAY
 
 The Highway Department is responsible fo
r the maintenance of 186.22 lane miles of ro
ad, all of which are paved. During the wint
er 
the highway is also responsible for 55 lane
 miles of state roads and 16 lane miles of
 county roads; altogether this means 24 pl
owing 
routes which requires 2.5 hours to complete. We provide such serv
ices as snow removal in the wi
nter, paving roads in the spring
, 
cleaning of 1,440 catch basins at the end of 
the winter season and leaf vacuuming the 
month of November. Also during the course
 of 
the year, we provide brush and leaf bag pi
ck up, installation and maintena
nce of guide rails, sweeping town roads, mowing town 
right 
of ways, and answering all calls from residents.  
2006 Accomplishments of the Department of Highway
 
 
•
 
Served as member of the Westchester/Putnam 
Counties of Town Superintendents of Highways  
•
 
Served as past President of the Westchester/Putn
am Counties of Town Superintendents of Highways 
•
 
Completed Work Zone Safety Training for 
all employees of the Highway Department 
•
 
Completed scheduled dr
ainage projects   
•
 
Purchased a paving machine to allow more roads to be pave
d in-house so that outside c
ontractor costs are decreased 
•
 
Recommended purchase of flex fuel vehicles to
 help improve the quality of our environment 
●
    Paved 12 lane miles of road 
 
2007 Goals of the Department of Highway
 
 
•
 
Serve as Chairman of the Board of Directors of the Westch
ester/Putnam Counties of Town Superintendents of Highways 
•
 
Implement safety training for all employees 
•
 
Expand drainage projects 
•
 
Pave 16 lane miles of road 
•
 
Continue with 7 year purchasing 
plan for vehicles and equipment 
•
 
Implement plan for new Highway Department garage

DEPARTMENT OF NORTH CASTLE PUBLIC LIBRARY
 
  Our mission is to serve as a community resource center for the 
intellectual, cultural and recreational needs of the citizens of
 North 
Castle. 
  
2006 Accomplishments of the Department
 of North Castle Public Library
 
 
•
 
Completed a Strategic Plan for the library through 2010 
•
 
Established an Express Collect
ion of popular fiction and DVDs 
•
 
Increased our holdings of popular
 titles and first-run movies 
•
 
Created an adult program series 
•
 
Increased programs for children 
•
 
Developed a newsletter 
•
 
Established community awarene
ss, promotion and programming 
•
 
Provided downloadable audi
o books from our home page, 
www.northcastlelibrary.org
 
  
2007 Goals of the Department of 
North Castle Public Library
 
 •
 
Migrate to a new online circulation system 
•
 
Update and remodel the Magazine 
Room, Circulation and Media areas 
•
 
Increase the number and variety 
of adult and children’s programs 
•
 
Redesign the library’s website 
•
 
Increase hours at the North White Plains Branch 
•
 
Update the collection and increase th
e number of popular materials, especial
ly best sellers, DVDs and audiobooks

 SEWER AND WATER DEPARTMENT
 
 
 The Sewer and Water Department is responsible for the operation of
 four (4) sewer districts, one of which includes a wastewater
 
treatment plant and five (5) water district
s.  Operation of all districts is covere
d 24/7 and 365 days per year.  There are ten
 (10) 
employees in the department that includes management and a s
ecretary.   Some of the distri
ct information is as follows: 
  
Sewer Districts

te Miles of Pipe

   All sewer districts are in
spected weekly; flow through the mainline sewers is
 verified by opening manholes
.  Pumping stations a
re also 
inspected weekly and readings are taken for pumping hours & cy
cles, emergency generator operati
on, etc.  Annually, all sewer ma
ins 
are jet cleaned and pumping stations vacuum cleaned to remove 
grease & grit accumulations.  The treatment plant & all stations 
are 
monitored  24/7  by  an  alarm  system  utilizi
ng  a  central  station.    The  wast
ewater  treatment  plant  in  Sewer  District  No.2  is  monito
red 
daily  where  all  of  the  necessary  samples  are  collected  and  analyzed  to  maintain  the  regulatory  standards  set  by  our  State  Pollu
tion 
Discharge  Elimination  System  (SPDES)  permit.    Currently,  an  upgr
ade  is  under  construction  at  the  
wastewater  treatment  plant,  th
e 
plant  design  flow  will  be  increased  from  380,000  gals/day  to
  450,000  gals/day.      Additionally,  further  upgrades  will  be  necessa
ry  to  
meet  mandatory  revisions  of  SPDES  permit  requirements,  which  re
late  to  nitrogen  removal  from  
the  Long  Island  Sound.    State  and  
County regulatory agencies perform ra
ndom inspections of the Plant monthly.

2006 Accomplishments of the Sewer and Water Department
 
 
•
 
Complied with all Federal and Stat
e requirements for drinking water 
•
 
Collected, analyzed, and reported daily, monthly, quart
erly and annul water samples for all districts 
•
 
Conducted semi-annual fire hydrant operation and 
maintenance checks in all water districts 
•
 
Replaced three (3) Windmill mechanisms for orname
ntal windmills (Water District No.2)   
•
 
Requested proposals from consultants to apply for Health
 Department approval for the reconditioning of both water 
storage tanks in Water District No. 1.  A consultant has b
een retained and we await He
alth Department approval to 
proceed. 
•
 
Hired consultants for a new water storag
e tank and production well in Water Dist
rict No. 2.  Both applications are 
before the Health Department and we 
expect to be out to bid shortly.  
•
 
Prepared, published, and distri
buted annual water quality repo
rts to all water customers 
•
 
Read water meters, prepared and dist
ributed bills for quarterly cycles. 
•
 
Updated water-billing software to integrat
e with radio water meter software syst
em.  Began installation of radio meter 
transmitters (Water District No.2 co
mplete, Water District No.5 -50%) 
•
 
Replaced three sewer pump station duplex pum
p control panels (Sew
er District No.3) 
•
 
Responded to and repaired various water main breaks and service line repairs 
•
 
Began GIS mapping of all sewer and 
water district infrastructure  
•
 
Hired a consultant for the revision of the 
Sewer District No.2 user fee ordinance 
in order to more accurately & fairly 
determine sewer use charge
s within the district. 
•
 
Applied for funding through the New York
 State Drinking Water Revolving Fund
